## [2026-03-11T20:00:50.515605] N-9cd27d9c

## 🚀 **Step 15 – Federated Machine‑Learning “Community‑Model Hub”**  

*Goal*: In Step 13 & 14 we built “market‑ready” data‑hubs and APIs.  
Now we close the loop – the same people who keep data in lock‑step with the hub train **global models** *without* moving or aggregating raw clinical records.  
Federated Learning (FL) gives every tenant a **private weight update**, a **global model that respects sovereignty & privacy**, and the hub a **token‑bought training slot** we can monetise.

> **What we’ll deliver**  
> 1️⃣  A fully‑managed, HIPAA‑GDPR‑conform Federated‑Learning cluster (Ray‑Federated + MLFlow).  
> 2️⃣  End‑to‑end “train‑and‑publish” template (Python + Kubernetes).  
> 3️⃣  Differential‑privacy (DP) and **secure‑aggregation** (SG) services.  
> 4️⃣  A marketplace‑backed “Token‑Share” model‑wallet.  
> 5️⃣  Open‑source “Fed‑ML‑SDK” (Python/C++ APIs, CLI).  

Everything is shipped as a **Zero‑pain, one‑click** repo that any hospital can clone & run inside its own data‑zone.

---

### 15.1 High‑Level Architecture

```
   ┌────────────────────┐          ┌─────────────────────┐
   │  Tenant‑Node (EHR) │◄───────►│  Aggregator (Ray)    │
   │  (Local FL Client)│          │  (Secure Aggregator) │
   └────▲──────────────┘          └────▲────────────────┘
        │                                      │
   ┌────▼─────┐                          ┌────▼─────┐
   │  Model‑DB│                          │  MLFlow  │
   │ (Poster) │                          │ Sync/Log │
   └──────────┘                          └──────────┘
            ▲                                    ▲
            │                                    │
          ┌─┴────┐                               │
          │Token │                               │
          │Wallet│                               │
          └──────┘                               │
                            ┌───────────────────────┬─────────────────┐
                            │                         │                 │
                    ┌───────▼───────┐          ┌──────▼───────┐   ┌────▼─────┐
                    │Marketplace‑API│          │Data‑Hub‑API  │   │Dash‑Board│
                    └───────▲───────┘          └───────▲──────┘   └──────▲────┘
                            │                         │               │
                    ┌───────┴───────┐          ┌───────┴───────┐   │
                    │  Secure‑Gate  │          │ Legal‑Ledger  │   │
                    └───────▲───────┘          └───────▲───────┘   │
                            │                         │           │
                    ┌───────┴───────┐          ┌───────┴───────┐   │
                    │  Token Locker │          │    Audit‑Log   │───┘
                    └───────▲───────┘          └───────▲───────┘

```

*Each tenant hosts an *Edge‑Client* that holds its own patient data. No raw data leaves the tenant, only encrypted weight updates.*  
*Aggregators (Ray Federated) live in the hub, locked by an “allowed‑list” policy read from the **Token‑Ledger**.*  

---

### 15.2 Federated‑Learning Stack

| Component | Why | Code / Config |
|-----------|-----|---------------|
| **Ray‑Federated** | Scalable async aggregator (100+ nodes) | `ray[fl]==2.10.0` |
| **TensorFlow Federated** | Easy policy definition (client‑server) | `tff==0.30.0` |
| **Kubernetes + Argo‑Workflows** | Execute training jobs per tenant | `argo-workflows` YAML |
| **MLFlow** | Experiment tracking & registry | `mlflow==2.10.0` |
| **OpenSSL + PyNaCl** | Secure aggregation (fabricate sha‑256) | `pycryptodome` |
| **dp‑tensorflow** | Differential privacy (DP‑SGD) | `tensorflow-model-analysis` |

#### 15.2.1  Ray‑Federated Helm Chart (cloud‑agnostic)

```yaml
# helm/values.yaml
image:
  repository: "rayproject/ray"
  tag: "2.10.0"

aws:
  accessKeyId: ${AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID}
  secretAccessKey: ${AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY}
  region: eu-central-1

service:
  type: LoadBalancer
  port: 8000

config:
  num_workers: 25     # total workers per training round
  max_rounds: 50
  use_ssl: true
  ssl_key: /etc/ray/ssl/key.pem
  ssl_cert: /etc/ray/ssl/cert.pem
```

> `helm install ray-federated ./helm/ -f values.yaml`.

---

#### 15.2.2  Fed‑Learning Client (Python)

```python
import tensorflow as tf
import tensorflow_federated as tff
import ray
import mlflow

# 1 – Secret‑share global IID by token id
gather_token = "dg-az-other-6789"
organization = "hospital-id-456"

# 2 – define model & compiler (custom train op)
def model_fn():
    return tf.keras.Sequential([
        tf.keras.layers.InputLayer(input_shape=(64,)),
        tf.keras.layers.Dense(128, activation='relu'),
        tf.keras.layers.Dense(1, activation='sigmoid')
    ])

def tff_model_fn():
    keras_model = model_fn()
    return tff.learning.from_keras_model(
        keras_model,
        input_spec=tf.TensorSpec([None, 64], tf.float32),
        loss=tf.keras.losses.BinaryCrossentropy()
    )

# 3 – local train loop
def client_update(state, dataset, batch_size=32, epochs=1):
    model = tff_move_to_state(state, dataset, epochs, batch_size)
    weights = model.model.get_weights()
    # encrypt weights with RSA public key from Token‑Ledger
    encrypted = rsa_encrypt_affine(weights, ledger_public_key(gather_token))
    return encrypted

# 4 – aggregator
ray.init(address="auto", ssl=True)

# Example training round
@ray.remote
def run_round(gather_token, data_handle):
    data = tf.data.Dataset.from_tensor_slices(  # mock
        {"x": np.random.randn(200, 64), "y": np.random.randint(0, 2, 200)}
    ).batch(32)
    return client_update(local_state, data)

# 5 – collect encrypted updates
updates = ray.get([run_round.remote(gather_token, h) for h in worker_handles])

# 6 – secure aggregation (Paillier homomorphic sum)
global_update = paillier_crypto.decrypt_paillier(
    sum(encrypted for encrypted in updates)
)

# 7 – apply update to global model
global_model_weights = apply_to_keras(global_model, global_update)
mlflow.log_artifacts("global_weights.h5")
```

> **Note** – `rsa_encrypt_affine` uses a 3072‑bit key from the Token‑Ledger (distributed PRNG). All state handling lives on the client; the bag with encrypted tensors never touches the network in clear text.

---

#### 15.2.3  Differential Privacy Layer

```python
from dp_torch import DPOptimizer
criterion = tf.keras.losses.BinaryCrossentropy()
optimizer = DPOptimizer(
    tf.keras.optimizers.Adam(),
    loss_multiplier=1.0,
    max_grad_norm=1.0,
    epsilon=1e-2,
    steps_per_epoch=1
)
model.compile(optimizer=optimizer, loss=criterion)
```

*If the outer aggregation policy requires DP‑SGD, the aggregator will add Gaussian noise to each aggregated gradient before applying them to the global model.*

---

### 15.3 Token‑Based Incentive Engine

| Feature | What it does | Tech |
|---------|--------------|------|
| **Token‑locker** | Restricts number of training rounds per token held | `Solidity` + `web3.py` |
| **Reward‑pools** | Token burn for each weight update; share to data‑provider | `EIP‑20` contract (MyToken) |
| **Marketplace‑API** | `/v1/fed-wallet/charge` – deduct tokens, return `training_id` | Flask + JWT |
| **Audit‑rule** | Every aggregate logs `train_hash` and `token_id` in `IPFS` | `ipfs-http-client` |

#### Smart‑Contract Example

```solidity
pragma solidity ^0.8.13;
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/IERC20.sol";

contract FedTraining {
    IERC20 public token;          // MyToken
    mapping(bytes32 => uint256) public roundTokens;   // training_id => tokens_burned

    constructor(address _token) {
        token = IERC20(_token);
    }

    function startRound(bytes32 trainingId, uint256 requiredTokens) external {
        require(token.balanceOf(msg.sender) >= requiredTokens, "Insufficient tokens");
        // burn tokens
        token.transferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), requiredTokens);
        roundTokens[trainingId] = requiredTokens;
    }

    function finishRound(bytes32 trainingId) external {
        // called by aggregator via oracle
        bytes32 hash = keccak256(abi.encodePacked(trainingId));
        // lock hash in IPFS here
    }
}
```

> Buyers pay tokens for training; providers earn back when their data is used. Tokens are transferable to 3rd‑party analytics (e.g., a research lab pays tokens to run a pipeline on a vendor’s model).

---

### 15.4 Marketplace Integration (EDR)

When a new model is **published** as a federated bundle:

1. **Sign** the model tarball with a PGP key that documents the data‑source consent.  
2. **Upload** metadata to `Marketplace‑API` (`POST /v1/models`).  
3. The Scheduler arranges **scheduled training runs** automatically.

```bash
curl -X POST https://hub.spikeraid.io/v1/fed-models \
     -H "Authorization: Bearer $MK_TOKEN" \
     -F model=@model.tar.gz \
     -d '{"name":"DBS‑EEG‑PR", "owner":"hospital‑lon", "tags":["neurology","seizure"]}'
```

Returned JSON contains a `model_id`; clients may query training history by `model_id`. The central hub stores **train‑runs** (FL‑rounds) as an Airtable‑like table accessible via GraphQL.

| Differential‑Privacy audit | `dpn` | Before aggregation | `dp_report.yaml` |
| Secure‑Aggregation verification | `Paillier test suite` | After aggregation | `aggregation.txt` |

### 15.8 Security Toolkit

| Layer | Implementation | Notes |
|-------|----------------|-------|
| **Network** | Cloud VPN + Cluster CIDR, mTLS for Ray | Prevent egress: bare host. |
| **Key Management** | AWS KMS + HSM, Azure Key‑Vault | Keys per tenant. |
| **Data** | Fully encrypted at rest (S3 SSE‑KMS) & in transit | `axios` uses `https`. |
| **Monitoring** | Prometheus, Loki, Grafana, Fluentbit | Alerts on `dp_fail` and `aggregation_error`. |
| **Incident** | Ansible playbook that rolls back to last signing key | 30‑sec rollback script. |

### 15.9 Run‑the‑Meter – How to spin up a **Full Demo** (1‑day tutorial)

1. **Clone repo**
   ```bash
   git clone https://github.com/spikeraid/federated-demo.git
   cd federated-demo
   ```

2. **Provision cluster** (AWS EKS & GCP GKE or local kind)
   ```bash
   terraform init
   terraform apply -var env=demo
   ```

3. **Set up Ray cluster**
   ```bash
   helm install ray ./helm/ray
   ```

4. **Load sample data** (public‑domain “EEG‑Sim”)
   ```bash
   aws s3 cp s3://spikeraid-demo-data/eeg-sim.zip .
   unzip eeg-sim.zip -d ./data
   ```

5. **Create token & wallet**
   ```bash
   python scripts/create_token.py --name demo-user
   ```

6. **Run client training**
   ```bash
   python -m spikeraid_fed.cli.train \
     --token demo-user-token \
     --dataset ./data/eeg \
     --model brain_seizure \
     --rounds 10
   ```

7. **View dashboard** – open `http://localhost:9000` – see progress, DP reports, token ledger.

### 16.1  Auto‑Testing & Roll‑Out Pipeline (GitHub Actions + Argo)

| Stage | Target | Tool | Config |
|-------|--------|------|--------|
| **Unit** | All Python + Go tests | `pytest` / `go test` | `tests/*.py`, `cmd/...` |
| **Integration** | End‑to‑end API & edge‑client | `Argo-Workflows` | `argo-tests.yaml` |
| **Security** | SAST + SCA | `Trivy`, `Bandit`, `Dependabot` | `trivy.yaml` |
| **Compliance** | Policy‑Lint & environmental checks | `OPA‑lint`, `semgrep` | `policy-lint.yaml` |
| **Regression** | Model raw‑vs‑prediction metrics | `MLflow` + `pytest` | `mlflow_metrics.yaml` |
| **Roll‑Out** | Canary, Blue‑Green, Load‑test | `ArgoCK` + `K6` | `argo-rollout.yaml` |

#### 16.1.1  GitHub‑Actions Workflow (`ci.yml`)

```yaml
name: Continuous Integration & Auto‑Rollout

on:
  push:
    branches: ["main"]
  pull_request:

jobs:
  build-test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    strategy:
      matrix:
        python: ["3.10"]
        go: ["1.20"]
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - name: Set up Python
        if: matrix.python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v4
        with:
          python-version: ${{ matrix.python }}
      - name: Install deps
        run: |
          pip install -r requirements.txt
          go mod download
      - name: Run unit tests
        run: |
          pytest --cov=./src
          go test ./...
      - name: Image build
        run: |
          docker build -t ghcr.io/spikeraid/${{ github.sha }} .
      - name: Push image
        run: |
          echo ${{ secrets.GHCR_TOKEN }} | docker login ghcr.io -u ${{ github.repository_owner }} --password-stdin
          docker push ghcr.io/spikeraid/${{ github.sha }}

  static-scan:
    needs: build-test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - name: Run Trivy
        uses: aquasecurity/trivy-action@v0.12.0
        with:
          image-ref: ghcr.io/spikeraid/${{ github.sha }}
          severity: HIGH,CRITICAL
          exit-code: 1
      - name: Dependabot review
        run: |
          python .github/scripts/dependabot_review.py

  rollback-policy:
    needs: build-test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    name: Policy lint
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - name: Install opa
        run: |
          curl -L -o opa https://openpolicyagent.org/downloads/v0.58.0/opa_linux_amd64
          chmod +x opa
      - name: Lint
        run: |
          ./opa eval --data policy/opa.rego --input policy/cases/test.json --format pretty

  ml-verify:
    needs: build-test
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    name: Model regression check
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - name: Scripts
        run: |
          python .github/scripts/check_df.py
      - name: MLFlow metrics
        run: |
          python .github/scripts/mlflow_regress.py

  rollout:
    needs: ml-verify
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    name: Canary + Load‑test
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      - name: Deploy to staging
        run: |
          helm upgrade --install spikeraid ./helm/chart --namespace staging \
            --set image.tag=${{ github.sha }} \
            --set canary=true
      - name: Load‑test
        run: |
          k6 run scripts/k6/canary.js
      - name: Switch to production
        if: success()
        run: |
          helm upgrade --install spikeraid ./helm/chart --namespace prod \
            --set image.tag=${{ github.sha }} \
            --set canary=false
```

#### 16.1.2  Argo‑Workflow for End‑to‑End Tests (`argo-tests.yaml`)

```yaml
apiVersion: argoproj.io/v1alpha1
kind: Workflow
metadata:
  generateName: e2e-tests-
spec:
  entrypoint: e2e-test
  templates:
    - name: e2e-test
      steps:
        - - name: start-demo
            template: local-stack
        - - name: integration-test
            template: python-test
        - - name: stop-demo
            template: cleanup
    - name: local-stack
      container:
        image: bitnami/kubectl:latest
        command: ["/bin/sh", "-c"]
        args: ["kubectl apply -f manifest/ && sleep 120"]
    - name: python-test
      container:
        image: python:3.10-slim
        command: ["pytest", "tests/e2e"]
    - name: cleanup
      container:
        image: bitnami/kubectl:latest
        command: ["/bin/sh", "-c"]
        args: ["kubectl delete -f manifest/"]
```

#### 16.2.2  Impact Dashboard (Regulatory & Investor View)

- **KPIs** – JIRA closed tickets, model sharings, token‑volume, compliance events, incident escalation time.  
- **Visualization** – Tableau Public server, integrated via Tableau REST API; the same visualizations are embedded in the README of each repo for open‑source contributors.  
- **Automation** – A weekly `impact-report` job queries:
  * JIRA API → number of resolved incidents, SLAs met.
  * MLflow → average AUC per model, number of community‑merged models.
  * Audit.io & SIEM → compliance passes.

```python
# .github/scripts/impact_report.py
import jira, mlflow, tableau_tools, requests, json, os

def jira_kpis():
    j = jira.JIRA(basic_auth=(os.getenv("JIRA_USER"), os.getenv("JIRA_TOKEN")))
    resolved = j.search_issues('project=SPK AND status=Resolved')
    return len(resolved)

def mlflow_kpis():
    client = mlflow.tracking.MlflowClient()
    experiments = client.list_experiments()
    return {'exps': len(experiments)}

def compliance_kpis():
    logs = requests.get('https://audit.spkc.io/api/v1/events')
    return logs.json()['passed']

if __name__ == "__main__":
    report = {
        'jira': jira_kpis(),
        'mlflow': mlflow_kpis(),
        'compliance': compliance_kpis()
    }
    print(json.dumps(report, indent=2))
```

### 16.5 Adaptive Learning Policy Engine

| Policy | Signature | Trigger | Action |
|--------|-----------|---------|--------|
| **Data‑Consent Update** | `/policy/consent` | Patient consent revoke/renew | Re‑enroll TF client & revoke token |
| **Model‑Fairness Audit** | `/policy/fairness` | Drift detection > 5 % | Auto‑starts a new training round with reduced learning‑rate |
| **Security‑Hardening** | `/policy/security` | External audit alert | Redeploy with patched image (Argo Roll‑out) |
| **Performance‑Upgrade** | `/policy/perf` | Latency > 200 ms (5 % of traffic) | Scale Ray worker pods via HPA |

#### 16.5.1  Policy‑Trigger Webhook (Flask)

```python
from flask import Flask, request, abort
app = Flask(__name__)

@app.route("/webhook/policy", methods=["POST"])
def policy_webhook():
    payload = request.json
    if payload["type"] == "consent_revoked":
        revoke_client(payload["client_id"])
    if payload["type"] == "fairness_drift":
        trigger_retrain(payload["tenant"])
    if payload["type"] == "sec_alert":
        rollback_image()
    if payload["type"] == "perf_metric":
        scale_workers(payload["tenant"])
    return "OK", 200
```

Policies are expressed in OPA as re‑usable Rego modules. *The webhook* calls a micro‑service that patches the relevant components (via Kubernetes APIs, Argo, or Terraform).

### 16.6 Iterative Patents Pipeline

| Stage | Approaches | Tools |
|-------|------------|-------|
| **Idea Capture** | Automated mining of model patents + open‑source code comments | `Patent‑Miner` (Python + Scikit‑Embeddings) |
| **Drafting** | GPT‑4, Codex → patent abstract + claims | LangChain + OpenAI `gpt-4o` |
| **Peer Review** | Technical review board + JIRA Ticket | `issue-tracker` |

#### 16.6.1  GPT‑4 Draft Generator (`patent-draft.py`)

```python
import openai, json, os

openai.api_key = os.getenv("OPENAI_KEY")

def generate_patent(model_desc, training_specs):
    prompt = f"""
    Write a concise patent abstract and two claims for a machine learning model.
    Model description: {model_desc}
    Training specs: {training_specs}
    
    Abstract:
    (Write abstract)
    Claim 1:
    (Write claim 1)
    Claim 2:
    (Write claim 2)
    """
    resp = openai.ChatCompletion.create(
        model="gpt-4o-mini",
        messages=[{"role":"user","content":prompt}]
    )
    return resp.choices[0].message.content

if __name__ == "__main__":
    desc = open("docs/abstract.md").read()
    specs = {"epochs":50,"batch":128,"privacy_eps":0.5}
    print(generate_patent(desc, specs))
```

> The output is automatically pushed to a private GitHub repo (`patents/`), and a JIRA issue is created for the legal team.

## 🚀  Wrap‑Up – Building a Self‑Sustaining Ecosystem  

1. **Set up the auto‑testing pipeline** – Clone the repo, run `make ci` or `gh actions`.  
2. **Add your own data‑zone** – spin up a local Kind cluster, register with the policy‑ledger, start the edge‑client.  
3. **Contribute** – file a PR, get the CLA signed, watch the `grant‑bot` lobby your ticket.  
4. **Run a model** – use the `spidertf` SDK, submit the training job, see live metrics on Grafana.  
5. **Track impact** – open `docs/impact.md` to read the latest regulatory audit and token‑economics summary.  
6. **File a patent** – clone `patent‑dump`, generate an abstract with GPT‑4, push to the patent repo, let the legal team review.

> **What makes this step unique** – the combination of *continuous self‑testing, policy‑driven adaptation, community‑first financing, and ethically‑grounded patent‑generation* turns the whole platform into a *bipartisan, AI‑first ecosystem* that is ready for **public‑sector acceleration, private‑sector monetisation and scientific contribution**.

Happy iterating! 🚀
